About the Board
An empowered Board comprising of diverse group of experienced leadership brings rich 
experience, strategic directions and takes the Company forward on path of sustainable 
progress. The Board is responsible for overseeing the management performance and 
governance of the Company on behalf of the shareholders and other stakeholders. The Board 
exercises independent judgement and plays a pivotal role in the oversight of the Company’s 
aﬀairs.
Board Composition and Leadership
Our Board of Directors combine Executive, Non- Executive and Independent Directors who 
contribute their deep expertise and insight. The Chairman, serving as Non- Executive Director, 
leads with forward- thinking vision and fairness. The Board members, who have served notable 
tenures, are key to guiding us towards achieving our objectives, promoting a culture of honesty 
and sustainable growth.
*For the purpose of Composition and other details of the above committees, please refer Annexure I- Corporate Governance Report forming part of
this Annual Report.

Board of Directors
Mr. Manish Dhanuka
(Managing Director)
Mr. Manish Dhanuka is the Director of Orchid Pharma Limited; he has the vision to 
rejuvenate Orchid Pharma Ltd. and take it on a fruitful path. His wide-ranging experience 
of handling operations, commercial, marketing and ﬁnance in the manufacturing 
industry provides for his analytical and decision-making skills facilitating the restoration 
of the company to its glorious past and to achieve even greater heights.
Mr. Ram Gopal Agarwal
(Chairman & Non-Executive Director)
Mr. Ram Gopal Agarwal is Founder Chairman of Dhanuka Group. His deep commitment and 
inspiring leadership in initial turbulent days is an example worth inculcating and his passion 
to contribute to Indian Agriculture is commendable. Mr. Ram Gopal Agarwal, Group 
Chairman, has been bestowed with many Awards for his tremendous contribution in Agro 
Industry like “Life Time Achievement Award” by Agri Business Summit and Agri Awards 2019, 
“Distinguished Contribution to Indian Agrochemicals Industry” during India Chem 2016 
International Conference organised by FICCI, National Safety Award – 3 times; Forbes Award 
– 200 Best under A  Billion Companies in Asia Paciﬁc etc.
Mr. Mridul Dhanuka
(Whole Time Director)
Mr. Mridul Dhanuka is a Chemical Engineer with a Master’s in Business Administration. He is 
associated with Dhanuka Group Ltd. since 2005. His technical expertise has supported to 
enlarge the product base of Dhanuka. Under his exuberant personality and ambitious 
leadership the Company was able to expand its production facilities, setting up a state of art 
world class manufacturing facility. He was responsible in successfully realigning the entire 
supply chain vertical from procurement to sales thereby making a positive contribution in the 
proﬁtability of the Company. He is a driving force behind the new business vertical of the 
Company.
Mr. Arjun Dhanuka
(Non-Executive Director)
Mr. Arjun Dhanuka started his career with Dhanuka Laboratories Limited in September 
2010. From 2010-13 his role comprised of managing all the purchase decisions of the 
Company. He graduated from Delhi University and is a now Whole Time Director with the 
Company. He strives in Business Development and strategy building. Mr. Dhanuka is 
young and dynamic person with an experience of more than a decade in the 
Pharmaceuticals Sector.

MANIFESTING
EXCELLENCE
Excellence is an ingrained practice achieved through persistent strategic evolution. Our 
global vision and strategy, paired with an ardent dedication to science and technology 
and a steadfast emphasis on precise execution and delivery, have propelled our growth 
trajectory. Our integrated business model empowers us to exploit opportunities across 
the pharmaceutical value chain, from research to the global delivery of therapeutics. 
Our discerning selection of therapeutic areas, combined with state-of-the-art 
infrastructure, establishes us as the partner of choice for pharmaceutical development 
and manufacturing. Our specialized product portfolio endows us with a distinct 
competitive advantage in the markets we serve. Strategic alliances enhance our market 
reach, enabling us to deliver superior quality products while securing improved 
margins. Our overarching strategy is to cultivate a sustainable organizational 
ecosystem, perpetually motivating individuals to reach unprecedented heights, 
empowering them to unearth new opportunities, and relentlessly focusing on 
honoring our commitments.

Message from
the Managing
Director
Dear Shareholders,
It gives me immense pleasure to present you this Report on the signiﬁcant performance of 
your Company during the year. 
As we move ahead we are taking the legacy of our journey to further strengthen the 
Company's position in the market and drive sustainable growth while creating value for 
stakeholders.
The global pharmaceutical industry witnessed a notable surge in spending in 2023, 
indicating a positive trajectory for medicine expenditure through 2028, fueled by strong 
growth in emerging markets.
On the highlights of Orchid’s journey through the F.Y . 2024, I am delighted to share that the 
year under review reﬂects the story of growth & expansion, discovery of new potential and 
tapping the earlier untouched opportunities while forging the path to success and setting 
the stronger pillars of a more stable organization with strong Compound Annual Growth 
Rate (CAGR) of 22% in sales and 30% in EBITDA over the past three years. We achieved sales 
of ₹819 crores, a signiﬁcant jump from ₹666 crores last year. Our full-year EBITDA stood at 
₹142 crores, up from ₹103 crores in FY2023. In my previous address, emphasis was on to 
strengthen our core and transform into a future ready organization. In the same direction, 
your Company has made major developments during the reporting period.

[[PAGE 13]]
The phase of revival has passed and the Company is performing better every passing quarter 
and year. During the ﬁscal, your Company had witnessed reduction of debts, positive trends 
in sales and operating proﬁts with higher margin than previous year and maintained 
eﬃcient plant operations. We have laid the foundation for continued growth with regular 
expansion in capacities and eﬀorts in Business Development. The investment in R&D in last 
few years is also likely to bear fruit in the coming years with new DMFs and Dossiers across 
the globe. The major projects like 7ACA and Ceﬁderocol are likely to propel us with a 
quantum leap once they get started.
I am extremely proud of the fact that, Orchid is the only Indian Company, ever to have 
invented a product which has received a New Drug Approval (NDA) from USFDA. Further, the 
European Medicines Agency have also granted approval to Orchid’s newly invented - 
Enmetazobactam. Due to the new invention of your Company, it stands out at global stage 
of Pharmaceutical Market. 
It is a signiﬁcant development in addressing the global need for aﬀordable and eﬃcacious 
drugs to combat Anti- Microbial Resistance (AMR). It is also a matter of great pride that as 
“the Pharmacy of the World”, India has ﬁnally developed a new drug for the ﬁrst time The 
recent approval of this product by the CDSCO, India has also paved the way for this product 
to be launched in India. As the issue of AntiMicrobial Resestance is very serious in India, this 
therapy will be a very important solution in the hands of the clinicians across the country. 
The company is making all eﬀorts to start manufacturing and commercial sales of the 
product at the earliest and make it available to the widest population. This product will also 
lay the foundation for the successful launch of the AMS Division, and the entry of Orchid 
Pharma in the Critical Sales Hospital market. We, the Orchidian’s are always at forefront to 
achieve and accomplish the targets with Governance in letter and spirit.  I would like to 
thank our Board of Directors for their relentless pursuit of excellence throughout the last 
ﬁnancial year.
We are grateful to the Central and State Governments and the Central DCGI and State FDAs 
for their support to the Company’s business plans. Your Board places on record their 
appreciation of the support provided by the customers, suppliers, services providers, 
medical fraternity and business partners.
We are thankful to the shareholders for their support and encouragement. The Directors 
and the Management acknowledges and are thankful to the employees for their immense 
commitment and contributions for the continual growth of the businesses and operations.
Manish Dhanuka
Managing Director

[[PAGE 14]]
PRIDE OF INDIA – ENMETAZOBACTAM
It is a proud moment for India, that Enmetazobactam, a Novel Beta Lactamase Inhibitor, the 
ﬁrst product invented in India by Orchid Pharma Ltd, has been cleared by European 
Medicine Agency and USFDA approval is also expected within February 2024.
The discovery of antibiotics was the inﬂection point in human existence since it contributed 
enormously to the extension of the human lifespan. Before the discovery of Penicillin, there 
was no mass cure for infections, and life expectancy around 1940 was 40 years. With 
Antibiotics, now infections are not just cured, but advanced procedures and treatments like 
surgeries are possible. This has taken the average lifespan to more than 70 years in the 
world.
Lately however, this healing is tempered due to AMR (Anti-Microbial Resistance) which is a 
signiﬁcant cause of more than 50 Lakhs deaths in 2019 worldwide as stated by leading 
bodies like WHO. 
AMR is a major topic of discussion in all health forums and is being called the silent 
pandemic by WHO. An illustration below shows how several species of bacteria have 
become resistant to the most common Antibiotics, some even to the extent of 80%. What 
this means is 8 out of 10 patients to whom Antibiotics are given, the drug is not able to cure 
them. By any means, this is a staggering number and a waste of public resources. 
This alarming threat and global spread of AMR has not been matched by the development 
of novel Anti-Microbials. The matter is so critical that WHO maintains a Global Priority List 
of Multi Drug Resistant (MDR) and even Carbapenems (the most eﬀective Antibiotics today) 
resistant strains, for which there is almost no simple cure today. 
[[PAGE 15]]
Safety proﬁle and broad-spectrum eﬃcacy have made Cephalosporins the most widely used 
antibiotics today.  Their extensive use has resulted in the emergence of resistant bacteria, a 
worldwide problem, with high prevalence in developing and developed countries. 
To treat these bacterial infections and maintain patient safety, Orchid Pharma identiﬁed a 
novel approach to lower the resistance of the bacteria, so that the older Antibiotics, which 
are safe and have a well-known eﬃcacy, but had stopped working due to AMR can start 
working again. It invented, Enmetazobactam, which in combination with Cefepime, has 
proven to be signiﬁcantly superior to the current standard of care. The need of the hour was 
a novel product, which can be the ﬁrst line of treatment for infections thereby reducing the 
total cost of healthcare which is extremely important for India.
ORCHID’s INVENTION
Enmetazobactam, a Novel Beta Lactamase Inhibitor, the ﬁrst product completely invented in 
India, which in combination with Cefepime (a safe and eﬀective 3rd Generation 
Cephalosporin Anti-Biotic) has proven to be signiﬁcantly superior to Piperacillin + 
Tazobactam (the current go-to drug for doctors), and is as potent as Meropenem.
It has been approved for complicated urinary tract infections (including pyelonephritis); 
hospital-acquired pneumonia (HAP), including ventilator associated pneumonia (VAP) and 
patients with bacteraemia associated with above.
The increasing proliferation of MDR bacteria and increasing AMR to most of the available 
drugs are leading to signiﬁcantly increased usage of expensive Carbapenems which are 
currently the only alternative. The need of the hour is a cost-eﬀective, Carbapenem sparing 
(saving the Carbapenems for future) therapy which can be used for infections.
Because of the demonstrated superiority of Cefepime + Enmetazobactam, combined with 
the inherent safety of the established molecule Cefepime, plus the beneﬁts of lower cost, 
better eﬃcacy and being Carbapenem sparing are enough reasons to quickly make this 
product the standard of care worldwide, especially in India. 
The fact that Enmetazobactam is not just Made in India but the ﬁrst drug to be Invented in 
India, makes the case for it that much stronger.

Business segments 
Your Company operates in one business segment viz., 
Cephalosporin pharmaceucals, in which the Company drives its 
major sales through Acve Pharmaceucal Ingredients (APIs). 
The Company is an established Export Oriented Unit (“EOU”) 
with porolio of anbiocs, both Human and Veterinary 
products. Anbiocs are life-saving drugs used to ﬁght infecons. 
Diﬀerent classes of anbiocs include Beta-lactam, Macrolide, 
Fluoroquinolone, Imidazole etc. Cephalosporins are beta-lactam 
anmicrobials used to manage various infecons from gram-
posive and gram-negave bacteria. The ﬁve generaons of 
cephalosporins are useful against skin infecons, urinary tract 
infecons, lower respiratory tract infecons, sexually 
transmied diseases, surgical prophylaxis, and other infecons 
like meningis.
We are pioneer producon of Quality Cephalosporins
16
[[PAGE 20]]
especially the sterile products, along with few veterinary 
products and are engaged in manufacturing and export of all ﬁve 
generaons of cephalosporin products. Amongst anbiocs, the 
company has one of the widest ranges of cephalosporin APIs, 
spanning all 5 generaons catering the need of various 
internaonal markets and is the one out of the only three USFDA 
approved facilies in the world. The Company has a strong global 
presence with a wide customer base.
Your Company is also engaged in manufacturing and export of 
general category ﬁnished dosage formulaons and an-infecve 
ﬁnished dosage formulaons through its formulaon facilies. 
Orchid is the only Indian Pharmaceucal Company, to ever have 
invented a New Chemical Enty (NCE, also colloquially called 
New Drug), which is approved in US and Europe. This product is 
called Enmetazobactam and it shall be launched in India shortly. 
Global launch is also expected this year itself on which your 
company is entled for royalty income.

Material events during the year under review
I.   Scheme of Merger/Amalgamaon:
During the year under review, the Scheme of Arrangement 
between M/s. Orchid Pharma Limited (“Transferee” or 
Amalgamated Company”) and M/s. Dhanuka Laboratories 
Limited (“Transferor” or “Amalgamang Company”) and their 
respecve shareholders and creditors ('Scheme') in compliance 
with Secons 230 to 232 and other relevant provisions of the 
Companies Act, 2013 (“Companies Act” or “the Act”) as reviewed 
and recommended by the Audit Commiee and Commiee of 
Independent Directors was approved by the Board. 
The implementaon of the aforesaid Scheme, which is subject to 
the approval of Shareholders and other Statutory authories 
would inter-alia enable both the transferor and transferee 
Companies to realize beneﬁt of greater synergies between their 
businesses, achieve wider product oﬀerings and geographical 
footprints, consolidate operaons thereby leveraging the 
capability of the Amalgamated company, yield beneﬁcial results 
and pool ﬁnancial resources as well as managerial, technical, 
distribuon and markeng resources of each other in the interest 
of maximizing value to their Shareholders and the Stakeholders 
with centralizaon of inventory and greater economies of scale. 
The Arrangement will ensure creaon of a combined enty under 
the Amalgamated Company, as the holding enty of the cluster, 
thereby resulng in on-me supplies, eﬃciency of management 
and maximizing value for the shareholders.
The Company has ﬁled the Scheme of Arrangement between 
M/s. Orchid Pharma Limited (“Transferee” or Amalgamated 
Company ”) and M/s. Dhanuka Laboratories Limited 
(“Transferor” or “Amalgamang Company”) and their respecve

Nominaon & Remuneraon Policy (NRC Policy)
The Company has formulated the Nominaon and Remuneraon 
Policy in compliance with Secon 178 of the Companies Act, 2013 
read along with the applicable Rules thereto and Part D of 
Schedule II of Securies and Exchange Board of India (Lisng 
Obligaons and Disclosure Requirements) Regulaons, 2015, as 
amended. The objecve of this policy is to ensure adequate and 
proper selecon and appointment of Directors, Senior 
Management Personnel and Key Managerial Personnel. NRC 
Policy determines the criteria of appointment to the Board and is 
vested with the authority to idenfy candidates for appointment 
to the Board of Directors. In evaluang the suitability of a person 
for appointment / connuing to hold appointment as a Director, 
the Nominaon and Remuneraon Commiee (“NRC”) takes 
into account apart from others, Board diversity, person's 
eligibility, qualiﬁcaon, skills, experse, track record, general 
understanding of the business, professional ethics, integrity, 
values and other ﬁt and proper criteria. Based on 
recommendaon of the NRC, the Board evaluates the 
candidate(s) and decides on the selecon of the appropriate 
member. In case of re-appointment of any Board member, NRC 
basis evaluaon scores of the concerned Board member 
pursuant to performance evaluaon, recommends its decision to 
the Board to extend or connue the term of appointment of the 
Board members. Addionally, NRC recommends to the board, 
remuneraon, in whatever form, payable to senior management. 
Further, it is aﬃrmed that the remuneraon to the Directors, Key 
Managerial Personnel and Senior Management Personnel is 
being ﬁxed based on the criteria and parameters menoned in 
the Nominaon and Remuneraon Policy of the Company.
The Policy is available on the website of the Company and the 
web-link for the same is
 hp://www.orchidpharma.com/downloads/Nominaon%20an
d%20Remuneraon%20Policy.pdf.

Environment
Environment management is the prime concern of Orchid 
Pharma Limited. Orchid has employed a state-of-the-art 
technology, zero liquid discharge (ZLD) treatment plant and world 
class treatment facilies for its liquid and gaseous pollutants 
generated from the producon processes. The zero discharge of 
liquid eﬄuent comprises of Membrane Bio Reactor, Reverse 
Osmosis, Solvent Stripping Column, Thermal Evaporaon & 
Agitated thin ﬁlm dryers (ATFDs) to treat the enre eﬄuent and 
recycle back into the system.
Waste Water Treatment
Low TDS eﬄuent is collected, equalized and neutralized into 
neutral pH and treated aerobically by Membrane Bio Reactor 
process comprising of aeropac equipped with jet aeraon system 
made up of Glass Fiber Reinforced Plasc / Original 
Hydrodynamic Aerators & Ultraﬁltraon System loaded with 
ceramic membrane. The permeate from ultraﬁltraon passes 
through reverse osmosis and disc RO plant to separate inorganic 
salts. The permeate of reverse osmosis and Disc RO plant are 
ulized in the cooling towers as make up water. The reject from 
the reverse osmosis plant is further treated in disc RO plant. The 
reject from the Disc RO plant is mixed with high total dissolved 
solids eﬄuent for further treatment. The excess bio mass from 
the aerobic system is centrifuged and sent to bio composng 
process to convert into useful manure.
High TDS eﬄuent is collected and neutralized into neutral PH. 
This eﬄuent is sent to Mechanical Evaporators (Single stage and 
three stage) to concentrate the salts to the level of 35%. Heat 
energy is recovered during the process of evaporang the 
eﬄuent and the recovered heat energy is ulized to reduce the 
energy consumpon. The concentrate from the evaporators are 
sent to Agitated Thin Film Dryers (ATFD) where it gets dried and 
the dried salt is collected at the boom of ATFD. The collected salt 
is bagged, stored in protected storage sheds and disposed as per 
hazardous waste authorizaon. 
Waste Air Treatment
The major emissions from the unit is from the boiler, power plant, 
producon process and powder processing area.
Process Scrubbers
Orchid installed process scrubbers in all producon blocks to 
treat the waste air generated from process reactors. 
Vent Gas Condensaon
 
Orchid installed vent gas condensaon system for fugive 
emissions from the storage tanks of solvents and secondary 
condensers of solvent recovery area to control the fugive 
emissions.
Reverse Jet Venturi Filter
Orchid installed reverse jet venturi ﬁlter to control the dust 
emission during the powder processing of bulk drugs. 
Adequate Stack Height
Adequate stack heights are provided for Steam Boiler and Power 
Plant for beer dispersion.
Electro Stac Precipitator (ESP) 
ESP is provided at the boiler emission to control the parculate 
maer.
Ambient Air Quality and Stack Emission Monitoring
Ambient air quality and stack emission monitoring is being 
carried out round the clock to check the emission level in the 
atmosphere.
Hazardous waste Management
Hazardous wastes are collected and stored in protected storage 
shed and disposed as per hazardous waste authorizaon.  
Bio composng
Bio sludge generated from the biological process of eﬄuent 
treatment are converted into useful compost.

th
World Environment Day was celebrated on 5  June, 2023 by 
planng trees with in our factory premises to create awareness 
on environment among employees.

MANAGEMENT DISCUSSION AND ANALYSIS REPORT
ECONOMIC OVERVIEW
GLOBAL
Global growth is projected at 3.1% in 2024 and 3.2% in 2025, as per World Economic Outlook (WEO) on account of greater-than expected 
resilience in the United States and several large emerging market and developing economies, as well as ﬁscal support in China. Despite 
facing challenges such as geopolical tensions, supply chain disrupons, and an energy crisis, the economy has maintained its strength 
and stability. The economic acvity remained robust, and the economy adapted well to changing ﬁnancial condions, leading to 
consistent job growth, strong consumer demand due to rising incomes, increased government spending, and higher labour force 
parcipaon.
Risks to the global outlook are now broadly balanced. On the upside, looser ﬁscal policy than necessary and assumed in projecons could 
raise economic acvity in the short term, although risking more costly policy adjustment later on. Inﬂaon could fall faster than expected 
amid further gains in labor force parcipaon, allowing central banks to bring easing plans forward. Arﬁcial intelligence and stronger 
structural reforms than ancipated could spur producvity and on the downside, new price spikes stemming from geopolical tensions, 
including those from the war in Ukraine and the conﬂict in Gaza and Israel, could, along with persistent core inﬂaon where labor 
markets are sll ght, raise interest rate expectaons and reduce asset prices. A divergence in disinﬂaon speeds among major 
economies could also cause currency movements that put ﬁnancial sectors under pressure. High interest rates could have greater cooling 
eﬀects than envisaged as ﬁxed-rate mortgages reset and households contend with high debt, causing ﬁnancial stress.
While some emerging markets have outperformed expectaons following robust domesc demand and improved trade dynamics, many 
developing economies connue to grapple with the dual pressures of high inﬂaon and increased external debt. This ﬁnancial strain 
limits their ability to invest in essenal sectors like healthcare and infrastructure, crical for sustainable growth.

witnessed in the previous FY 2022-23. Moreover, the Interim Budget for FY 2024-25 announced by the government indicates a move 
towards a self-reliant India, diminishing dependence on imports and fostering growth in domesc industries. By maintaining the status

INDUSTRY OVERVIEW
GLOBAL
30
[[PAGE 34]]
Global health systems have demonstrated remarkable resilience in the face of the pandemic, global inﬂaon, and regional conﬂicts and 
have moved forward to adopt novel therapies and increase usage. Overall, the global use and spending on medicines is exceeding pre-
pandemic growth rates and is expected to connue signiﬁcantly above those trends through 2028. The pharmaceucal industry has and 
will connue to have a signiﬁcant impact on the global economy in terms of contribuon to GDP. 
The Pharmaceucals market has been growing steadily in recent years, which is mainly driven by innovave drugs and an increasing 
demand for drugs and treatments worldwide. The largest driver of medicine spending growth through the next ﬁve years is sll expected 
to be the availability and use in developed markets of innovave therapeucs and oﬀset by losses of exclusivity and the lower costs of 
generics and biosimilars. Tradionally, innovave medicine growth has occurred most in the years immediately following launch, 
whereas recent years and the forecast outlook show growth driven by older products. This mix of spending growth between volume-
driven growth, and mix-driven changes in the cost of therapy are showing most geographies shiing to more expensive therapies, 
reﬂecng the broader availability and paent access to medicines with higher clinical value.

INDIAN
The Indian Pharmaceucal industry is currently ranked third in pharmaceucal producon by volume aer evolving over me into a 
thriving industry growing at a CAGR of 9.43% since the past nine years. India has highest number of pharmaceucal manufacturing 
facilies that comply with the US Food and Drug Administraon (USFDA) and has 500 API producers that make for around 8% of the 
worldwide API market.
Indian pharmaceucal sector supplies over 50% of global demand for various vaccines, 40% of generic demand in the US and 25% of all 
medicine in the UK. The domesc pharmaceucal industry includes a network of 3,000 drug companies and ~10,500 manufacturing 
units. India enjoys an important posion in the global pharmaceucals sector. The country also has a large pool of sciensts and 
engineers with a potenal to steer the industry ahead to greater heights.
Indian pharmaceucal industry is known for its generic medicines and low-cost vaccines globally. Transformed over the years as a vibrant 
sector, presently Indian Pharma ranks third in pharmaceucal producon by volume. The Pharmaceucal industry in India is the third 
largest in the world in terms of volume and 14th largest in terms of value. The Pharma sector currently contributes to around 1.72% of the 
country's GDP.
32
[[PAGE 36]]
Source: Pharmaceucals Industry Report, May 2024, Indian Brand Equity Foundaon
Market size of India pharmaceucals industry is expected to reach US$ 65 billion by 2024, ~US$ 130 billion by 2030 and US$ 450 billion 
market by 2047. According to the government data, the Indian pharmaceucal industry is worth approximately US$ 50 billion with over 
US$ 25 billion of the value coming from exports. About 20% of the global exports in generic drugs are met by India.
The pharmaceucal industry in India is a signiﬁcant part of the naon's foreign trade and oﬀers lucrave potenal for investors. Millions 
of people around the world receive aﬀordable and inexpensive generic medicaons from India, which also runs a sizable number of 
plants that adhere to Good Manufacturing Pracces (GMP) standards set by the World Health Organizaon (WHO) and the United States 
Food and Drug Administraon (USFDA). 
The Indian Government has taken many steps to reduce costs and bring down healthcare expenses. The Naonal Health Protecon 
Scheme, which aims to oﬀer universal healthcare, the ageing populaon, the rise in chronic diseases, and other government 
programmes, including the opening of pharmacies that oﬀer inexpensive generic medicaons, should all contribute to boost the Indian 
pharmaceucal industry. Some of the iniaves taken by the Government to promote the pharmaceucal sector in India are; 1) The 
government earmarked Rs. 1,000 crore (US$ 120 million) for the promoon of bulk drug parks for FY25, a signiﬁcant increase from the 
previous year; 2) The total outlay for the development of the pharmaceucal industry for FY25 was increased to Rs. 1,300 crore (US$ 
156.5 million) while the budget for the promoon of medical device parks was raised to Rs. 150 crore (US$ 18 million) for FY25.; 3) The 
allocaon for assistance to medical device clusters for common facilies (AMD-CF) was pegged at Rs. 40 crore (US$ 4.1 million) for FY25.; 
4) The outlay for the Jan Aushadhi scheme, the iniave to provide aﬀordable generic medicines in the country, was hiked to Rs. 284.5 
crore (US$ 34 million) for FY25, up from Rs. 110 crore (US$ 13 million) in the revised esmate for FY24.
OPPOURTUNITIES AND THREATS
The future holds both opportunity and increased complexity for the pharmaceucal industry. 
Growth Drivers
1.   Strong Demand: Rising income, greater health awareness, lifestyle diseases and increasing access to insurance will contribute to 
growth. The healthcare sector, as of 2024, is one of India's largest employers, employing a total of 7.5 million people. A recent 
research report predicts that the integraon of Arﬁcial Intelligence (AI) within the Indian healthcare sector will create nearly 3 
million new jobs by 2028.
2.   Aracve Opportunies: India's public expenditure on healthcare touched 2.1 % of GDP in FY23 and 2.2% in FY22, against 1.6% in 
FY21, as per the Economic Survey 2022-23. Two vaccines Bharat Biotech's Covaxin and Oxford- AstraZeneca's Covishield 
manufactured by the Serum Instute of India) were instrumental in medically safeguarding the Indian populaon and those of 100+ 
countries against COVID-19.
332023-24
Annual Report
[[PAGE 37]]
3.   Policy and Government support: The Government aims to develop India as a global healthcare hub. Public health surveillance in 
India will further strengthen the health systems. In the Interim Union Budget 2024-25, the government allocated Rs.90,659 crore 
(US$ 10.93 billion) to the Ministry of Health and Family Welfare (MoHFW). In March 2021, the Parliament passed the Naonal 
Commission for Allied & Healthcare Professions Bill 2021, which aims to create a body that will regulate and maintain educaonal and 
service standards for healthcare professionals.
4.  Rising Manpower: Availability of a large pool of welltrained medical professionals in the country. The number of allopathic doctors 
with recognised medical qualiﬁcaons (under the I.M.C Act) registered with state medical councils/naonal medical council 
increased to 1.3 million in November 2021, from 0.83 million in 2010.
5.  Growing space: This industry is sll in its growing phase, according to the Life Cycle of an Industry. So, it can sll go a long way and 
spread its wings further apart. So, it can sll take control over the market, given the right markeng and adversing.
6.   Foreign investment: Per India's Consolidated FDI Policy, foreign direct investment in the pharmaceucal sector in greenﬁeld (new) 
projects is permied up to 100% without the approval of the Department of Pharmaceucals (the “DoP”). 100% FDI in the 
pharmaceucal sector is allowed in brownﬁeld pharmaceucals; wherein 74% is allowed under the automac route and thereaer 
through the government approval route.
7.   Connued Rise of Digital Health: The rise of digital health technologies will transform the pharma industry. Paents will be able to 
monitor their health remotely through telemedicine and wearables. This technology will also provide valuable data for pharma 
companies to develop more eﬀecve treatments
Threats for Pharmaceucal Industry
1.  Demand for a skilled workforce: The pharmaceucal industry requires a workforce that has signiﬁcant knowledge, experience, and 
skills. Training the workforce helps to acquire the necessary skills to ensure, enhance and improve their parcipaon in their daily 
tasks. This will also help to ﬁll in any skills gaps that may be observed in the workforce.
2.   Supply chain disrupon: Supply chains have witnessed an unprecedented disrupon all around the world, and this represents one of 
the major challenges facing the pharmaceucal industry. Many pharma companies are looking to supply chain innovaons and 
circular supply chain models to tackle these challenges and build business resilience.
3.  Regulatory compliance: Pharmaceucal companies must comply with various regulaons, from clinical trial requirements to 
manufacturing and distribuon standards. Keeping up with these regulaons can be daunng, and failure to comply can result in 
costly ﬁnes and reputaonal damage.
4.   R&D Costs: Developing new drugs and treatments is an expensive and me-consuming process. With rising R&D costs and increasing 
pressure to deliver results, pharmaceucal companies must be able to streamline their research processes and opmize their 
resources.
5.   Intellectual property
      The pharmaceucal industry is highly compeve, and intellectual property is crical to the success of any company. Protecng and 
enforcing patents can be a complex and costly process, and the threat of patent infringement is a constant concern.
6.    Pricing pressure
    Pharmaceucal companies face increasing pressure to control the cost of their products, both from government regulators and 
consumers. This pressure can lead to lower proﬁt margins and increased compeon, making it harder for companies to invest in 
R&D and bring new products to market.
7.    Supply chain management
   The pharmaceucal supply chain is complex and highly regulated, with mulple stakeholders involved in drug producon, 
transportaon, and distribuon. Ensuring the safety and quality of pharmaceucal products at every stage of the supply chain is 
essenal but can be challenging.

API manufacturing
API is an important segment of the Indian pharma industry, contribung to around 35% of the market. The Indian pharmaceucal 
industry is the 3rd largest in terms of volume and 13th largest in terms of value. Furthermore, India also has the highest number of US-  
FDA compliant Pharma plants outside of USA and is home to more than 3,000 pharma companies with a strong network of over 10,500 
manufacturing facilies as well as a highly skilled resource pool, occupying forty-four percent of global abbreviated new drug 
applicaons, the API industry's growth in the country has been fuelled by adopng global standards. 
There are 500 API manufacturers contribung about 8% in the global API Industry. India is the largest supplier of generic medicines. It 
manufactures about 60,000 diﬀerent generic brands across 60 therapeuc categories and accounts for 20% of the global supply of 
generics. Access to aﬀordable HIV treatment from India is one of the greatest success stories in medicine. Because of the low price and 
high quality, Indian medicines are preferred worldwide, making it “pharmacy of the world”. 
There is a growing investment in R&D for API producon processes, as well in quality, environment and safety, resulng in beer, safer 
and cleaner technologies and substanal increase of pharmaceucal technology know-how and intellectual property. Companies should 
increase their size to face global compeon. Mergers and acquisions are taking place in order to pursue globalisaon projects. API 
manufacturers ask for global harmonised regulaons with an increasingly strong relaonship between the EU and the USA.
RISKS AND CONCERNS
1.   Regulaons and direcves: Pharma manufacturing is already highly regulated, with manufacturers connually reﬁning their 
processes to overcome substanal compliance obligaons and costs. Unfortunately, compliance will become even more 
challenging in the coming years. The U.S. Food and Drug Administraon (FDA) recently issued pharma industry guidance documents 
that impact how organizaons collect, manage and submit quality data. 
2.     Talent shortage: As with all sectors, pharma manufacturers are struggling to aract the right talent. According to , 80% of McKinsey
facilies are having diﬃculty ﬁnding candidates with the right skills. Pharma manufacturers are rapidly introducing new 
technologies, automang processes, and applying advanced analycs to data. Disrupons such as new business models and 
enrely new product modalies are contribung to the current landscape.
3.    Cybersecurity and data protecon: Cybersecurity threats are a major concern for nearly every industry, but according to IBM's 
latest report, pharma ranks among the top three industry vercals for the highest average cost of a data breach. In 2023, the global 
average cost of a data breach in the pharma industry was an eyewatering $4.82 million.
4.    Cost and inﬂaonary pressures: Inﬂaon is sll nearly triple what it was pre-pandemic, and it will connue to play a role in 2024. 
According to Deloies 2024 Health Care and Life Sciences Outlook, 36% of survey respondents indicated that the economy and 
inﬂaon would connue to inﬂuence their strategy. This is seemingly at odds with the commonly held belief that pharma is one of 
the few industries that is 'recession-proof,' and can beer withstand economic downturns and pressure than other industries.
Addionally, the broader risks associates with the business of the Company are:
• Our business is dependent on the success of our relaonships with our customers and sale of our products to them. Any adverse 
developments or inability to enter into or maintain such relaonships, loss of such customers, the deterioraon of their ﬁnancial 
condion or prospects, or a reducon in their demand for our products could adversely aﬀect our business, results of operaons, 
ﬁnancial condion and cash ﬂows.
• We derive majority of our revenue from our sterile and oral cephalosporin API business, of which a limited number of key products 
generate a signiﬁcant poron of our total revenue, and our business may be adversely aﬀected if our sterile and oral cephalosporin 
API business or our key products do not perform as well as expected or if substute products become available or gain wider market 
acceptance.
• Manufacture, storage and supply of oral and sterile cephalosporin APIs is a complex process and if we fail to maintain the quality of 
our sterile APIs manufacture at any stage, our business and operaons will be adversely aﬀected.
• We are dependent on select countries and few suppliers for signiﬁcant part of our raw material. Any delay, interrupon or shorall in 
the supply of our raw materials or an increase in our raw material costs, or other input costs, may adversely aﬀect the pricing and

         supply of our products and have an adverse eﬀect on our business, results of operaons and ﬁnancial condion.
• Our business subjects us to risks in mulple countries that could materially adversely aﬀect our business, cash ﬂows, results of 
operaons and prospects. We also face foreign exchange risks that could adversely aﬀect our results of operaons and cash ﬂows.
• Our inability to successfully ulize our installed capacity, could have an adverse eﬀect on our business, results of operaons,

• Pricing pressure from customers may aﬀect our gross margin, proﬁtability and ability to increase our prices, which in turn may

Corporate Governance Report
In Compliance with Regulaon 34(3) read with Schedule V of the Securies and Exchange Board of India (Lisng Obligaons and Disclosure 
requirements) Regulaons, 2015 and amendments made thereunder the Company submits the Corporate (“SEBI Lisng Regulaons”),

A.   COMPANY'S PHILOSOPHY ON CODE OF CORPORATE GOVERNANCE 
Corporate Governance is a key for creang and maximising the stakeholder value. At Orchid, we believe that eﬀecve corporate governance 
pracces plays a pivotal role in growing a sustainable and successful business, creang a long-term value for the Stakeholders. Orchid ﬁrmly 
believes in adhering to Corporate Governance code to ensure protecon of its investor's interest as well as healthy growth of the Company. The 
philosophy of your Company is to adhere to TRANSPERANCY, INTEGRITY and ACCOUNTABILITY. We focus to achieve and maintain the highest 
standard of Corporate Governance by providing adequate and mely informaon to all the shareholders and recognizing the rights of its 
shareholders and encouraging co-operaon between the Company and the stakeholders comprising regulators, employees, customers, 
vendors, investors and the society at large. We believe in performance oriented systems. Over the years, your Company's Corporate Governance 
framework has evolved a lot with development of some best corporate working and behavior.
B.   BOARD OF DIRECTORS  
I)     Composion and Category of Board
An empowered Board comprising of diverse group of experienced leadership brings rich experience, strategic direcons and takes the Company 
forward on path of sustainable progress. The Board is responsible for overseeing the management performance and governance of the 
Company on behalf of the shareholders and other stakeholders. The Board exercises independent judgement and plays a pivotal role in the 
oversight of the Company's aﬀairs. 
The composion of Board of Directors of the Company is in conformity with the requirements of Regulaon 17 and Regulaon 17A of SEBI Lisng 
Regulaons as well as provisions of the Companies Act, 2013 (“the Act”) and the Arcles of Associaon of the Company and all other applicable 
laws and in accordance with the best pracces of Corporate Governance.

iii)   Board Meengs & Aendance Record of the Directors
In Compliance with the Companies Act, 2013 and SEBI Lisng Regulaons, minimum four pre-scheduled Board meengs were held. Addional 
Board meengs were convened by giving appropriate noces to address the Company's speciﬁc needs. Dates of Quarterly Board Meengs are 
ﬁxed in advance and agenda papers are circulated to Directors generally one week before the meeng except for meengs which are held on a 
shorter noce period. Video conferencing or other audio visual facilies are used to facilitate Directors who were unable to aend meengs 
physically at the venue, to parcipate in the meengs. In case of exigencies or urgencies, resoluons were considered and passed by circulaon 
as well.
The Board is given presentaons covering the Company's major business segments and their operaons, business environment, the Company's 
business areas, including business opportunies and strategy and risk management pracces before taking on record the Company's quarterly/ 
annual ﬁnancial results/statements.

day), July 12, 2023, August 10, 2023, November 04, 2023, December 06, 2023, December 16, 2023 and February 08, 2024. Time gap between 
any two consecuve board meengs was not more than 120 days. The previous Annual General Meeng of the Company was held on August 09, 
2023.

C.   COMMITTEES OF THE BOARD OF DIRECTORS
The Board Commiees play a crucial role in the Governance structure of the Company and have been assigned speciﬁc areas/acvies that need 
closer review. They are set up under the formal approval of the Board, to carry out their clearly deﬁned roles. Currently, the Board has the 
following Statutory Commiees viz., Audit Commiee, Nominaon and Remuneraon Commiee, Stakeholders' Relaonship Commiee, Risk 
Management Commiee, Corporate Social Responsibility Commiee. Also, the Non-Statutory Commiees of the Company during the period 
under review were Banking, Finance & Operaons Commiee, Business Restructuring Commiee, Capital Restructuring Commiee and 
Restructuring Commiee. Details of the roles, composion and terms of reference of these Commiees are determined by the Board and their 
relevance is reviewed from me to me.
I.   Audit Commiee
The Company has a qualiﬁed and independent Audit Commiee which has been formed in pursuance of Regulaon 18 of the Lisng Regulaons 
and Secon 177 of the Act. The primary objecve of the Commiee is to monitor and provide eﬀecve supervision of the management's 
ﬁnancial reporng process to ensure accurate and mely disclosures, with the highest level of transparency, integrity and quality of ﬁnancial 
reporng. It oversees the work carried out in the ﬁnancial reporng process by the management, the internal auditors and the independent 
auditors. All possible measures have been taken by the Commiee to ensure the objecvity and independence of the independent auditor. The 
Company Secretary of the Company acts as the Secretary to the Commiee. All members of the Commiee are ﬁnancially literate and have 
accounng or related ﬁnancial management experse.

b)   Terms of Reference:
Primarily, the Audit Commiee is responsible for:
1.   Oversight of the Company's ﬁnancial reporng process and the disclosure of its ﬁnancial informaon to ensure that the ﬁnancial statement is 
correct, suﬃcient and credible.
2.   Recommendaon for appointment, remuneraon and terms of appointment of auditors of the Company.

[[PAGE 52]]
3.  Approval of payment to statutory auditors for any other services rendered by the statutory auditors.
4.   Reviewing, with the management, the annual ﬁnancial statements and auditors report thereon before submission to the Board for approval, 
with parcular reference to:  
a.   Maers required being included in the Director's Responsibility Statement to be included in the Board's Report in terms of clause (c) of sub-
secon 3 of Secon 134 of the Companies Act, 2013
b.   Changes, if any, in accounng policies and pracces and reasons for the   same 
c.   Major accounng entries involving esmates based on the exercise of judgment by management. 
d.   Signiﬁcant adjustments made in the ﬁnancial statements arising out of audit ﬁndings.
e.   Compliance with lisng and other legal requirements relang to ﬁnancial statements
f.    Disclosure of any related party transacons
g.   Modiﬁed opinion(s) in the dra audit report.
5.   Reviewing, with the management, the quarterly ﬁnancial statements before submission to the Board for approval.
6.   Reviewing, with the management, the statement of uses / applicaon of funds raised through an issue (public issue, rights issue, preferenal 
issue, etc.), the statement of funds ulized for purposes other than those stated in the oﬀer document/prospectus/noce and the report 
submied by the monitoring agency monitoring the ulisaon of proceeds of a public or rights issue, preferenal  issue or qualiﬁed instuons 
placement and making appropriate recommendaons to the board to take up steps in this maer.

8.   Approval or any subsequent modiﬁcaon of transacons of the company with related pares; including the omnibus approval for the 
related party transacons proposed to be entered by the Company.

10.  Valuaon of undertakings or assets of the Company, wherever it is necessary.
11.  Evaluaon of internal ﬁnancial controls and risk management systems.
12.  Reviewing, with the management, performance of statutory and internal auditors and adequacy of the internal control systems.
13.  Reviewing the adequacy of internal audit funcon, if any, including the structure of the internal audit department, staﬃng and 
seniority of the oﬃcial heading the department, reporng structure coverage and frequency of internal audit.
14.  Discussion with internal auditors of any signiﬁcant ﬁndings and follow up there on.
15.  Reviewing the ﬁndings of any internal invesgaons by the internal auditors into maers  where  there  is  suspected fraud  or  irregularity  
or  a  failure  of  internal control systems of a material nature and reporng the maer to the board.
16.  Discussion with statutory auditors before the audit commences, about the nature and  scope  of  audit  as  well  as  post - audit  discussion to  
ascertain  any  area  of concern
17.  To look into the reasons for substanal defaults in the payment to the depositors, debenture holders, shareholders (in case of non - payment 
of declared dividends) and creditors 
18.  To review the funconing of the whistle blower mechanism.
19.  Approval   of   appointment   of   chief   ﬁnancial   oﬃcer   aer   assessing   the qualiﬁcaons, experience and background, etc. of the 
candidate.
20.  Carrying out any other funcon as is menoned in the terms of reference of the audit commiee.
21.  Reviewing  the  ulizaon  of  loans  and/or  advances  from/investment  by  the holding company in the subsidiary exceeding  rupees

II.  Nominaon and Remuneraon Commiee (NRC) 
The Nominaon and Remuneraon Commiee funcons in accordance with Secon 178 of the Act, and Regulaon 19 of the Lisng 
Regulaons. It discharges the Board's responsibilies of Nominaon and Remuneraon of the Company's Execuve/Non-Execuve Directors 
Senior Management and Key Managerial Personnel. It also has the overall responsibility of approving and evaluang the performance of 
individual directors, Board as a whole and Board Commiees.

Companies Act, 2013 and Regulaon 19 of the SEBI Lisng Regulaons. Chairman of the NRC is an Independent Director. The Company Secretary 
acts as the Secretary to the Nominaon and Remuneraon Commiee.

d)   Appointment of Directors and Key Managerial Personnel 
NRC determines the criteria of appointment to the Board and is vested with the authority to idenfy candidates for appointment to the Board of 
Directors. In evaluang the suitability of a person for appointment / connuing to hold appointment as a Director, the NRC takes into account 
apart from others, Board diversity, person's eligibility, qualiﬁcaon, skills, experse, track record, general understanding of the business, 
professional ethics, integrity, values and other ﬁt and proper criteria's. Based on recommendaon of the NRC, the Board evaluates the 
candidate(s) and decide on the selecon of the appropriate member. In case of re-appointment of any Board member, NRC on the basis of 
evaluaon scores of the concerned Board member pursuant to performance evaluaon, recommends its decision to the Board to extend or 
connue the term of appointment of the Board member.
The authority to idenfy right candidates for appointment of KMP's and SMPs (Senior Management Personnel) is vested with the Execuve 
Directors. The Execuve Directors along with HR Head idenﬁes candidates internally or externally and proposes to NRC for its approval for 
appointment along with proposed remuneraon. The remuneraon proposed used to be consistent with the strategy of the Company and 
in line with the comparable market & internal remuneraon benchmarks.

V.   Performance Evaluaon criteria for Independent Directors
In terms of provisions of the Companies Act, 2013 and Regulaon 17(10) read with Regulaon 25(4) of SEBI Lisng Regulaons, the Board 
conducts an annual performance evaluaon of its own performance, the performance of the Directors individually as well as the evaluaon of 
the working of its Commiees through quesonnaire designed with qualitave parameters and feedback based on rangs. The Performance 
evaluaon of Independent Directors was carried out on an annual basis. Structured assessment forms were used for the evaluaon of 
Independent Directors comprising various aspects relevant to their funconing such as aendance at the Board and Commiee meengs and 
acve parcipaon thereof, ﬂow of informaon to the Board, objecve judgement, adherence to the Code of Conduct, eﬀecveness of 
contribuon and its impact on the Company, performance of speciﬁc dues and obligaons, governance, etc. The Board of Directors expressed 
their sasfacon with the Policy and Annual Performance Evaluaon process and evaluaon results.

As a holding company, the performance of subsidiaries is monitored by the following means:
• The Audit commiee / Board of the Company quarterly reviews the ﬁnancial statements of the unlisted subsidiary companies, in parcular 
the investments made by these companies.
• Minutes of Board meengs of the unlisted subsidiary companies are placed before the Board meengs of the Company periodically.
• A statement containing all signiﬁcant transacons and arrangements entered into by the unlisted subsidiary companies is placed at the 
Board meengs of the Company periodically.
• Since the Company does not have any material unlisted Indian subsidiaries of the Company, Secretarial audit is not required to be carried 
out for the period under review.

circulars dated 20th April, 2018 read with circular dated November 03, 2021, November 17, 2023, December 27, 2023, June 10, 2024 and Master

Specimen  Signature details by all shareholders to the Registrar and Transfer Agent of the Company.

shareholders for submission of their PAN, Nominaon, Contact Details, Bank Account details for registraon / update in the prescribed formats.

As per the approved Resoluon plan, your Company has issued 14,300 Zero Coupon, unsecured, Oponally Converble Non-marketable 
Debentures of Rs.1,00,000/- aggregang to Rs.143 Crores to M/s. Dhanuka Laboratories Limited. The tenor of the OCDs shall be Ten (10) years or 
such further period as may be mutually discussed between the Company and OCDs holder. In case, the OCD holders exercise their opon to 
convert the same, then the said conversion shall happen only on the basis of face value of each of the OCD and no interest shall be payable to the 
OCD holders. However, if the OCD holders opt not to exercise their opon for conversion, then the OCD holders shall be entled to redempon 
premium of at least 11 % IRR on annual basis on the amount of the said OCDs or such higher amount as the Board decides aer considering the 
market price of shares of the Company and the achievement of EBIDTA; However in any case, redempon premium shall not exceed beyond 18% 
IRR on an annual basis. The said OCD, ll the me it is not converted into equity shares, shall not be listed on any stock exchange in India and are 
permied to be transferred only with the permission of the Board of Directors of the Company. Further, there shall be no redempon of OCDs, 
including payment of interest/ other kind of return of what so ever nature thereon, unl enre outstanding of the loan availed from Union Bank 
of India is paid in full to the lender. The OCD holder, any me during the tenor shall have the right to convert whole or any part of OCDs into equity 
shares of Rupees 10/- each at par of Company (“OCD Conversion shares”) and accordingly, each OCD of Rupees One Lakh will be converted into 
10,000 equity shares having face value of Rs.10 each (“OCD Conversion rao”).

OTHER DISCLOSURES
 i)   Materially Signiﬁcant Related Party Transacon
Transacons with the Related Pares has been disclosed in the Notes to the Financial Statements for F.Y. 2023-24, also the Company has 
submied a report on all related party transacons entered into by the Company on consolidated basis on half yearly periodicity as per the 
format prescribed in the relevant accounng standards to NSE and BSE within prescribed meline of publicaon of the standalone and 
consolidated Financial Results. 
The Company has not entered into any materially signiﬁcant related party transacon that may have potenal conﬂict with the interests of the 
Company at large. The Board of Directors have approved and adopted a “Policy on Materiality of Related Party Transacons (RPT) and dealing 
with RPT” and the same has been uploaded on the website of the Company and can be accessed at
 hp://www.orchidpharma.com/downloads/RPT.pdf
All the contract/arrangements/transacons entered into with Related Pares as per the Act and Regulaon 23 of the SEBI Lisng Regulaons 
during the Financial Year 2023-24 were in ordinary course of business and on an arm's length basis and do not aract provisions of Secon 188 of 
the Act. The required statements / disclosures with respect to the related party transacons are placed before the Audit Commiee on regular 
basis. 
Further, the details of material transacons with the related pares at arm's length basis during the year is disclosed in Form – AOC – 2 annexed 
to the Board' Report of this Annual Report.
Details of non-compliance by the listed enty, penales, strictures imposed on the listed enty by stock exchange(s) or the board or any 
statutory authority, on any maer related to capital markets, during the last three years; 
I.   During the Financial year 2019-2020, there was a delay in compliance with Regulaon 6(1) of the SEBI (LODR) Regulaons, 2015 as the 
Company was unable to appoint the Company Secretary within the spulated melines and hence a ﬁne of Rs.75, 520/- by the NSE Limited 
and Rs.75,520/- by BSE Limited in this regard was imposed during the ﬁnancial year 2020-2021. The above ﬁnes have been remied by the 
Company to the Stock exchanges. The Company had made necessary representaons to NSE and BSE for the waiver imposed and during the 
year under review, BSE Limited has waived oﬀ the ﬁne imposed towards non- compliance with Regulaon 6(1) of the SEBI (LODR) 
Regulaons, 2015;
II.   Pursuant to implementaon of the Resoluon Plan approved by Hon'ble Naonal Company Law Tribunal under secon 31 of the 
Insolvency and Bankruptcy Code, 2016 (31 of 2016), the Company was required to bring the public shareholding to twenty-ﬁve per cent 
within a maximum period of three years from the date of such fall, in the manner speciﬁed by the Securies and Exchange Board of India. 
752023-24
Annual Report
2023-24
Annual Report
[[PAGE 79]]
In an eﬀort to maintain minimum public shareholding at 25%, the Company made an oﬀer for sale, in which the promoter of the Company, i.e., 
Dhanuka Laboratories Limited, sold 32,80,115 equity shares, represenng 8.04% of equity shareholding in the Company in the year 2021. Aer 
the oﬀer for sale, the promoter of the Company held 90% of the equity shares, bringing the public shareholding to 10% within 18 months from 
the date of acquiring the Company. 
Further, the Company iniated the process of raising capital by qualiﬁed instuonal placement to meet the condion of Minimum Public 
Shareholding of 25% by issuing addional shares though QIP. However due to poor market condions the same could not be achieved as on 
March 31, 2023.
In view of the above, the Company had submied applicaon to SEBI and Naonal Stock Exchange of India Limited and BSE Limited (collecvely 
herein referred as Stock Exchanges) under Regulaon 102 of the SEBI Lisng Regulaons along with applicable fee and requested for extending 
the meline by one year to meet the MPS requirement. However, Stock Exchanges vide its leers dated April 20, 2023 iniated the acon to 
freeze the shareholding of Promoters as SEBI Circular No. CFD/CMD/CIR/P/2017/115 dated October 10, 2017, for non-compliance in meeng 
the minimum public shareholding requirement laid under Regulaon 38 of SEBI (Lisng Obligaons and Disclosure Requirements) Regulaons, 
2015 and further directed the Promoters, Promoter Group and Directors of the Company not to hold any new posion as director in any other 
listed enty ll the date of compliance.
As per the aforesaid SEBI Circular, the Stock Exchanges levied penalty of Rs. 5,000/- plus GST on the Company for period ended March 31, 2023. 
Further, the Stock Exchanges vide their leer dated July 06, 2023, has levied penalty of Rs. 5,19,200/- towards non-compliance for a period 
commencing from April 01, 2023 ll June 26, 2023. The Company paid the same.
 
However, the Board of Directors at its meeng held on June 27, 2023, approved the allotment of 99,02,705 Equity Shares on Qualiﬁed 
Instuonal Placement basis in accordance to the SEBI (Issue of Capital and Disclosure Requirement) Regulaons read with applicable 
provisions of Companies Act, 2013. Pursuant to the aforemenoned allotment of Equity Shares, the paid-up equity share capital of the Company 
stands increased from ₹ 408,164,000 comprising of 40,816,400 Equity Shares to ₹ 50,71,91,050 comprising of 5,07,19,105 Equity Shares. 
Consequently, the Promoter Shareholding in the Company stands decreased from 89.96% to 72. 40% whereas the Public shareholding 
increased from 10.04% to 27. 60% w.e.f. June 27, 2023.
The Company is in compliance with the requirement of Minimum Public Shareholding as per Regulaon 38 of SEBI LODR Regulaons read with 
Rule 19A (5) of SCRA Rules w.e.f. June 27, 2023.
ii)   Vigil Mechanism and Whistle Blower Policy
The Company promotes ethical behaviour in all its business acvies and has put in place a mechanism in the form of Whistle Blower Policy 
(“Policy or Mechanism”) for directors and employees of the Company to report concerns about unethical behaviour, actual or suspected fraud 
or violaon of the Company's code of conduct or ethics policy and any leak/suspected leak of Unpublished Price Sensive Informaon. The 
Policy is applicable to all the Directors of the Company, permanent & contractual employees of the Company based in India or outside, 
employees of other agencies deployed for the company, contractors, vendors, suppliers or agencies (or any of their employees), customers of 
the Company and any other person having an associaon with the Company. The Policy also provides for adequate safeguards against 
vicmizaon of director(s) / employee(s) who avail the Policy and also provide for direct access to the Chairman of the Audit Commiee. No 
person has been denied access to the Audit Commiee.
The Whistle blower Policy adopted by the Company may be accessed at
 hp://www.orchidpharma.com/downloads/Policy%20on%20%20Whistle%20Blower.pdf
iii)   Commodity price risk, Foreign Exchange Risk and Hedging Acvies
A signiﬁcant part of the Orchid's revenue, costs, assets and liabilies are denominated in foreign currencies. Further, the Company does not have 
any signiﬁcant exposure in commodies directly and does not carry out any commodity hedging acvies. However, Unhedged trade and 
ﬁnancial exposure thus creates potenal to adversely impact its operaons and overall proﬁtability.
iv)   Dividend Distribuon Policy:
The Company has adopted a Dividend Distribuon (Policy) which deﬁnes the ﬁnancial parameters and factors, be considered for declaraon and

v)   Details of ulizaon of funds raised through preferenal allotment or qualiﬁed instuons placement as speciﬁed under Regulaon 32 
(7A) of SEBI Lisng Regulaons
Pursuant to Secons 23, 42, 62 of the Companies Act, 2013, the rules framed thereunder and all relevant provisions of the Memorandum and 
Arcles of Associaon, SEBI (Issue of Capital and Disclosure Requirements) Regulaons, 2018 read with SEBI Lisng Regulaons, as amended, 
the Board of Directors at its meeng held on December 01, 2022 approved to raise funds through issuance of Equity Shares of the Company for a 
Face Value of Rs. 10/-  each, in one or more tranches, in such form and manner and upon such terms and condions as the Board may in its 
absolute discreon deem appropriate, on Qualiﬁed Instuonal Placement (QIP) basis, for an aggregate amount not exceeding Rs. 500 Cr. 
(Rupees Five Hundred Crores only) to eligible Qualiﬁed Instuonal Buyers.
In the maer, the shareholders passed a special resoluon at the extra-ordinary general meeng of the Company held on December 29, 2022 
and approved the QIP process. The Company launched Preliminary Placement Document and Placement Document on June 22, 2023 and June 
27, 2023 respecvely.
The Company obtained In-Principle approval on June 22, 2023 and consequently, the Board of Directors at its meeng held on June 27, 2023, 
approved the allotment of 99,02,705 Equity Shares on Qualiﬁed Instuonal Placement basis in accordance to the SEBI (Issue of Capital and 
Disclosure Requirement) Regulaons read with applicable provisions of Companies Act, 2013. Pursuant to the aforemenoned allotment of 
Equity Shares, the paid-up equity share capital of the Company stands increased from ₹ 408,164,000 comprising of 40,816,400 Equity Shares to 
₹50,71,91,050 comprising of 5,07,19,105 Equity Shares.
Further, the Company obtained Lisng and trading approval on the aforesaid allotment of equity shares on June 30, 2023.
The informaon w.r.t the ulisaon of funds raised through QIP, in accordance with Regulaon 32(7A) of SEBI Lisng Regulaons, as cerﬁed by 
the Statutory Auditors of the Company, forms part of the Audited Financial Results declared by the Company for the period ended on March 31, 
2024 of F.Y. 2023-24 and same has been submied to the Stock Exchanges with availability on the website of the Company at below link:

The company is deeply committed to ethical conduct, ﬁrmly 
opposing pracces such as bribery or kickbacks and acvely 
discouraging employees and stakeholders from engaging in 
such behaviour. We maintain a strict zero -tolerance policy 
against bribery and corrupon, dedicated to uph olding our 
business relaonships with professionalism, fairness, and 
ethical integrity. To eﬀecvely combat bribery, we have 
implemented comprehensive guidelines and stringent control 
measures. We have clearly deﬁned consequences for 
misconduct and encourage employees to promptly report any 
wrongdoing. Our corporate governance pracces are 
anchored in principles of honesty and integrity, ensuring full 
compliance with legal and regulatory requirements. The 
principles of an-corrupon and bribery are captured in 
codes of conduct of the Company. Furthermore, we provide a 
Whistleblower mechanism for reporng any concerns or 
instances of misconduct.

2  Does the enty have processes in 
place to avoid/ manage conﬂict of 
interests involving members of the 
Board? (Yes/No)  
 If yes, provide details of the same.  
Yes, every Director of the Company annually discloses any concerns or 
interests they have in the Company or other enes, including any changes, 
which encompass shareholding interests. Addionally, Directors annually 
declare under the Code of Conduct that they will always act in the Company's 
best interests and ensure that any other business or personal associaons do 
not conﬂict with the Company's operaons or their role. During Board 
meengs, Directors abstain from parcipang in i tems where they have a 
concern or interest. To idenfy and monitor conﬂicts of interest involving the 
Directors and Key Managerial Personnel (KMPs), the Corporate Secretarial 
team maintains a database of the Directors and the enes in which they are 
interested. This list is shared with the Finance department to monitor and 
track transacons entered into by the Company with these pares. 
Addionally, the Senior Management aﬃrms annually that they have not 
engaged in any material ﬁnancial and comme rcial transacons that could 
potenally conﬂict with the Company's interests.

the stakeholder relaonship, which further facilitates the idenﬁcaon of key material issues impacn g the 
Company’s growth. The stakeholder engagement and materiality assessment exercise conducted in FY 2023 -24 led 
to the priorizaon of material issues, mapping of the risks relevant to each material topic, and development of 
consequent risk migaon steps. The primary outcome of the stakeholder engagement exercise resulted in the 
idenﬁcaon and priorizaon of material issues relevant to environmental, social, governance, and economic 
aspects. The idenﬁed material issues were presented to th e highest governing member and the Board for their 
feedback and guidance on strategizing the sustainable growth model of the Company. As part of the Company’s 
eﬀorts to connually engage with internal and external stakeholder g roups for the idenﬁcaon of key material 
issues impacng them, the stakeholder engagement exercise undergoes periodic review.

5.
 
Has the enty implemented a 
mechanism for Zero Liquid 
Discharge? If yes, provide details of 
its coverage and implementaon. 
Mechanism 
implemented?(Yes/No):
 
The company employs a zero liquid discharge (ZLD) eﬄuent trea tment plant, 
which includes advanced components such as a Membrane Bio Reactor 
(MBR), Reverse Osmosis (RO) system, Solvent Stripping Column, Thermal 
Evaporators (both Single Eﬀect and Mulple Eﬀect Evaporators -
 
MEE), and a 
Dryer plant. These integrated
 
technologies treat the enre volume of trade 
eﬄuent, allowing for its complete recycling back into the ulity processes.

8.
 
Does the enty have any project 
related to reducing Green House Gas 
emission? If Yes, then provide details. 
Have project? (Yes/No): Yes
 
Yes, the Company is acvely engaged in several projects aimed at reducing 
greenhouse gas (GHG) emissions. These
 
iniaves focus on conserving 
energy and opmizing resource usage within our manufacturing facilies. 
Below are some key measures we have undertaken:
 
 Energy Conservaon Iniaves:
 Power Consumpon Reducon: We have enhanced our manufacturing 
processes to boost energy eﬃciency, beneﬁng all products in terms of 
power consumpon per kilogram of Acve Pharmaceucal Ingredients 
(APIs).
 
 Water and Energy Reducon Measures: While our products do not 
broadly aﬀect consumer energy and water usag e, we consistently work on 
reducing our own consumpon. 
 Eﬄuent Streamlining: We are opmizing eﬄuent streams to lower energy 
usage in our Ecology plant. For example, halng certain operaons has led 
to notable energy and steam savings. 
 
Operaonal Controls: Implemenng operaonal controls for pumps and 
fans based on plant operaons has resulted in further energy savings. 
 
Cooling Circuit Opmizaon: 
We have achieved considerable energy savings by opmizing power 
consumpon in our cooling circuits, using temperature-based cutoﬀs for 
radiator fans, improving Plate Heat Exchangers (PHE), and managing chill 
water pumps according to plant needs. 
 
Renewable Energy Trials: 
We are experimenng with renewable energy fuels, such as rice husk, in 
our  boilers to reduce coal usage, eﬀecvely replacing a poron of our coal 
consumpon. 
 
Insulaon Improvements: 
Improving insulaon in our refrigeraon systems has led to signiﬁcant 
energy savings. 
 
Compressed Air and Nitrogen Leak Prevenon:
 
Conducting air audits and addressing leaks in compressed air and nitrogen 
systems across our plant has resulted in substanal energy savings.
 
 
Solvent Recovery Facility:
 
Our solvent recovery facility, equipped with mulple disllaon columns, 
extracts, washes, and recovers impure solvents from API producon. A 
large percentage of the feed is recycled as pure/recovered solvent, 
reducing the need for fresh solvents. The disllaon process is managed 
through advanced systems like DCS, PLC, and SCADA, ensuring safety and 
eﬃciency. The puriﬁed solvent mixtures are then reused in producon, 
minimizing the requirement for new solvents.

10
 
Brieﬂy describe the waste management 
pracces adopted in your 
establishments. Describe the strategy 
adopted by your company to reduce 
usage of hazardous and toxic chemicals 
in your products and processes and the 
pracces adopted to manage such 
wastes.
 
Yes. The Company operates a highly eﬃcient solvent recovery facility equipped with 
thirty disllaon columns and addional extracon and washing capabilies. Impure 
solvents and mother liquor collected from API producon are processed, with about 95% 
of the feed being recycled back as pure, recovered solvent.
The aqueous layer resulng from the disllaon is directed to the Eﬄuent Treatment 
Plant for further processing. Solid wastes generated during disllaon are collected and 
disposed of in compliance with regulaons. The solvent recovery process is meculously 
controlled through advanced systems like DCS, PLC, and SCADA, which manage 
temperature, ﬂow, and pressure with integrated process and safety interlocks. Solvent 
storage tanks and systems are nitrogen-blanketed for safe operaon.
In the solvent recovery facility, solvent mixtures are separated from the mother liquor 
using various types of disllaon columns (atmospheric and vacuum), operated at 
diﬀerent temperatures based on the boiling points of the solvents. Aer disllaon, 
the puriﬁed solvents are collected in a tank and reused in producon, thereby 
reducing the need for fresh solvents.

Business Connuity Plans (BCP) are essenal for ensuring that a company can 
recover from disrupons in producon acvies. For manufacturing plants, the 
speciﬁc objecves of BCP include idenfying various threats that could interrupt 
business operaons. It involves establishing advanced arrangements and 
procedures that enable rapid response to emergencies, ensuring the connuous 
operaon of crical business funcons. The plan aims to minimize employee 
injuries, prevent loss of life, and reduce damage and losses. It also focuses on 
protecng essenal facilies, equipment, vital records, and other assets.
BCP requires idenfying crisis response teams and outlining their speciﬁc 
responsibilies. Eﬀecve decision-making processes are crucial to restoring 
operaons promptly. The plan includes idenfying alternave acons to migate 
or minimize the eﬀects of the crisis and shorten response mes. It quanﬁes the 
potenal impact of various events in terms of ﬁnancial loss, me, business 
disrupon, and workforce impact. The goal is to ensure a swi recovery from 
emergencies and the mely resumpon of full-scale manufacturing. Addionally, 
maintaining the quality of products, protecng the customer base, and preserving 
the company's brand during emergencies are crical components of the plan.

(Pursuant to Secon 134(3)(m) of the Companies Act, 2013 and Rule 8(3) of the Companies (Accounts) Rules, 2014 
 
 
 
(a) Energy Conservaon Measures Taken   
 
The following energy conservaon measures were taken by your Company during the year under review at its 
manufacturing facility in Alathur  
 
v Installed new energy eﬃcient Chiller with Variable Frequency Drive (VFD) for Ulity-3 plant in place of Kirloskar 
centrifugal chiller with an annual saving of 8 lakh units. 
 
v Installed new energy eﬃcient VFD based -10 deg C 150 TR brine chiller in place of exisng old chiller with an annual 
saving of 5.625 lakh units. 
 
v Replacement of Microbiology plant air cooled chiller by energy efﬁcient water cooled chiller thereby a saving of 66 lakh 
units per annum saving is achieved. 
 
v Installed energy eﬃcient Eﬄuent treatment system to reduce thermal & electrical energy consumpon for an annual 
saving of 11.25 lakh units. 
 
Due to the implementaon of various energy conservaon acvies menoned in (a) above, the annual saving is 33.123 lakh 
units which contributed in reducon of emission of CO2 by approx. 2700 tons this year. 
 
(b) Proposed energy conservaon measures 
 
Some of the proposals that are considered / being implemented for saving energy are: 
 
v Exploring of Low temperature Mechanical Va pour Recompression in ETP plant. 
 
v  Installaon of VFD for exisng +7 Water cooled Kirloskar Chiller & Frick chillers for -25 deg C brine circulaon system. 
Expected annual saving – 5.625 lakh units. 
 
v Installaon of VFD based Atlas Air compressor for plant air requirements. Expected annual saving – 7.25 lakh units. 
 
v Proposal of Water cooled VFD based chiller in place of Air cooled chiller in Ph6 & 14. Expected annual saving – 12.75 lakh 
units. 
 
130
[[PAGE 134]]
v Installaon of STP for reducing the eﬄuent treatment cost. Expected annual saving – 3.75 lakh units. 
 
v Proposal of -40 deg C VFD based brine chilling plant for process requirement. Expected annual saving – 4.75 lakh units. 
 
v Other proposals are also being explored for energy saving.  
 
Further, the energy conservation measures proposed to be taken up by the company as menoned in (b) above are expected 
to bring in savings of around 34.125 lakh units annually which will result in reducon of emission of CO2 by approx. 2800 tons 
yearly.  
 
(c) The steps taken by the Company for ulizing alternate sources of energy 
 
v Capve Power Purchase of electrical energy through Hybrid (Wind cum Solar) Power plant for an annual energy 
consumpon of 180 Lakh units per annum has resulted in a saving of 44.62 lakh units per annum resulng to reducon of 
emission of CO2 by 3650 tons yearly. The alternate energy is 43% of the annual energy consumpon of the Company. 
 
(d) Capital Investment on Energy Conservaon Equipment: 
 
Rs. 276 Lakhs of capital investment was made on energy conservaon equipment during the Financial Year 2023-24.

Annexure VII  to the Board’s Report – Technology Absorpon 
 
I. Chemistry & Technology Development  
 
Chemistry & Technology Development (CTD) Centre is also called R&D. A specialized department is responsible 
for developing new technologies and techniques for the manufacturing process for new Cephalosporin both 
oral & injectable APIs and also for new molecules. 
 
Orchid’s experse encompass the enre spectrum of Pharmaceucal R&D 
1. Biotech R & D 
2. Synthesis R&D including Polypepde synthesis Division 
3. Analycal R&D 
4. Intellectual Property Management 
5. Connuous Improvement R&D  
 
All these combined; give us the strength and ability to ﬁle First to File Para- IV applications for US and other 
regulatory markets.
 
 
Orchid’s CTD has
 
extended its support to new customers and new markets on intellectual property review, FTO 
analysis, invalidaon/non-infringement of IP approach, apart from generaon of scienﬁc and evidence-based 
jusﬁcaons to meet the requirement of regulatory agencies and ensure smooth IP pathway.  
 
 
Orchid’s CTD
 
has also supported internal and external customers by generang and providing standards of 
related substances and working standards for substanang the dossiers ﬁled and manufacturing of APIs.  
Evaluaon of New monograph and veriﬁcaon studies were carried out to permit API and formulation business 
in various markets. 
 
 
In order to meet the emerging requirement from regulatory agencies from across the
 
world, CTD
 
has taken up 
comprehensive analysis of the manufacturing processes of Cephalosporin

scienﬁc data.
