Notes to the Consolidated Financial Statements for  the year ended March 31, 2024
(All amounts are INR lakhs, except share data and unless otherwise stated)
Show cause noces issued by various Government authories are considered for evaluaon of conngent liabilies only 
when converted into demand.
Conngent assets
Where an inﬂow of economic beneﬁts is probable, the Group discloses a brief descripon of the nature of the conngent 
assets at the end of the reporng period, and, where praccable, an esmate of their ﬁnancial eﬀect.
Conngent assets are disclosed but not recognised in the ﬁnancial statements.
q) Cash and cash equivalents
Cash comprises cash on hand and demand deposits with banks. Cash equivalents are short-term balances with original 
maturity of less than 3 months, highly liquid investments that are readily converble into cash, which are subject to 
insigniﬁcant risk of changes in value.
r) Cash Flow Statement
Cash ﬂows are presented using indirect method, whereby proﬁt / (loss) before tax is adjusted for the eﬀects of 
transacons of non-cash nature and any deferrals or accruals of past or future cash receipts or payments.
Bank borrowings are generally considered to be ﬁnancing acvies. However, where bank overdras which are 
repayable on demand form an integral part of an enty's cash management, bank overdras are included as a 
component of cash and cash equivalents for the purpose of Cash ﬂow statement.
s) Non current assets held for sale and disposal groups
The Group classiﬁes a non-current asset (or disposal group) as held for sale if its carrying amount will be recovered 
principally through a sale transacon rather than through connuing use. Immediately before the inial classiﬁcaon of 
the asset (or disposal group) as held for sale, the carrying amounts of the asset (or all the assets and liabilies in the 
group) shall be measured in accordance with applicable Ind ASs.
The Group measures a non-current asset (or disposal group) classiﬁed as held for sale at the lower of its carrying amount 
and fair value less costs to sell. The Group does not depreciate (or amorse) a non-current asset while it is classiﬁed as 
held for sale or while it is part of a disposal group classiﬁed as held for sale. Interest and other expenses aributable to 
the liabilies of a disposal group classiﬁed as held for sale shall connue to be recognised.
t) Earnings per share
The basic earnings per share are computed by dividing the net proﬁt for the period aributable to equity shareholders 
by the weighted average number of equity shares outstanding during the period.  
Diluted EPS is computed by dividing the net proﬁt aer tax by the weighted average number of equity shares considered 
for deriving basic EPS and also weighted average number of equity shares that could have been issued upon conversion 
of all diluve potenal equity shares. Diluve potenal equity shares are deemed converted as of the beginning of the 
period, unless issued at a later date. Diluve potenal equity shares are determined independently for each period 
presented. The number of equity shares and potenally diluve equity shares are adjusted for bonus shares, as 
appropriate.

Capital reserve on Amalgamaon was created in the earlier years as per 
approved resoluon plan on the amalgamaon of the special purpose 
vehicle with the Group. The Group can use this reserve for issuing fully 
paid up Bonus shares.
Securies Premium was credited when shares are issued at a premium. 
The Group can use this reserve to issue bonus shares, to provide for 
premium on redempon of shares or debentures, preliminary expenses 
and the commission paid or discount allowed
General Reserve represents the statutory reserve in accordance with 
Companies Act, 2013 wherein a poron of proﬁt is apporoned to 
general reserve. This is a free reserve and the Group can use it for 
declaraon of dividends, subject to the condions prescribed by the 
Companies Act2013
This represents the equity component accounted as per split accounng 
prescribed for compound ﬁnancial instruments under Ind AS 109 
"Financial Instruments". This will be available as free reserves once the 
Group completed the conversion of the debentures into equity.
Capital reserve was created in the earlier years in respect of business 
acquired by the Group. The Group can use this reserve for issuing fully 
paid up Bonus shares.

Foreign Currency Fluctuaon Reserve represents the cumulave impact 
of translang the ﬁnancial statements of foreign subsidiaries. The same 
will be reclassiﬁed to the proﬁt and loss account on disposal of 
investments in those subsidiaries in accordance with Ind AS 21.
Other comprehensive income represents the balance in equity for items 
to be accounted in Other Comprehensive Income (OCI). The Group has 
opted to recognise the changes in the fair value of certain investments in 
equity instruments and remeasurement of deﬁned beneﬁt obligaons in 
OCI. The Group transfers amounts from this reserve to Retained Earnings 
in case of actuarial loss / gain and in case of fair value recognion of 
equity instrument, the same will be transferred when the respecve 
equity instruments are derecognised.

The Group is in the process of discussion  with the Lessor  for the out of court selement.

such public announcement, the IRP/ RP of the Corporate Debtor has received certain claims from statutory authories
which was admied under the provisions of Insolvency and Bankruptcy Code, 2016 (IBC code) and subsequent
selement made as per the approved resoluon plan. Accordingly, the Corporate Debtor/ Resoluon Applicant/ SPV will
have no addional exposure arising out of the claims towards the Statutory Dues which have not been admied and/or
the claims which have been rejected (partly or fully) by the RP and/or because of the re-classiﬁcaon in the category of
creditor(s).
Considering the above, all statutory liabilies of the Holding Company of pre-CIRP period is considered as completely
 seled and no liability, whatsoever, including conngent in nature is exisng on implementaon of the resoluon plan.

Claims against the Group not acknowledged as debts
The Group has taken certain lands on lease for its operaons in respect of which the lease agreement expired before the
date of commencement of the Corporate Insolvency Resoluon Process. As part of the right to review the exisng
agreements, the Group has made a detailed assessment of the market rent for the property and the market value of the
property for outright purchase. Since the present rent as per erstwhile lease agreements is signiﬁcantly high
considering the market value of the property itself, the Group is in talks with the lessor for renewal of the lease with
lower rent or for outright purchase of the property as part of the implementaon of the resoluon plan. However, no
ﬁnality is reached on this maer as of date. 
Pending compleon of the negoaon, the Company has disputed the poron of the lease rent, considered to be
excessive than the market rate, amounng to Rs.3,871.68 Lakhs upto March 31, 2024 (FY : 2022-23 Rs. 3,077.00 lacs)in
respect of the aforesaid lease. Based on the legal opinion obtained, the management is of the opinion that no liability will 
arise on compleon of negoaon.

45 Terms and condions of borrowings
A) Long term borrowings - Term loans from banks
1) Foreign Currency Term Loan:
As per the terms of the Loan agreement, Interest for the Foreign Currency Term Loan (FCTL) is @ 6 Months SOFR plus 2.00% margin. 
This Loan is repayable in 14 equal quarterly installments starng from December 2021. However, in July 2023 the Group had prepaid 
and fully seled the said Loan.
2) Rupee Term Loan :
Rupee term loan of Rs.5,000 Lakhs was sanconed during the ﬁnancial year 2022-23  with the terms of interest @ 8.36% per annum 
linked with 3 months T bill with a tenor of 54 months including  a moratorium of 12 months from ﬁrst disbursement.  The outstanding 
amount of Rs. 3205.16 lakhs has been prepaid in the month of July 2023 out of QIP funds.
The above Loans were  secured by way of :
i) Exclusive charge on the moveable ﬁxed assets of the Company funded out of the Term Loan by way of hypothecaon, both 
present and future
ii) First pari passu charge over
a) all other movable ﬁxed assets of the Company by way of hypothecaon,  both present and future
b) Immovable Fixed Assets by way of mortgage of land/ leasehold rights and all the buildings of the Company at Alathur, both 
present and future
c) all the rights, tles, interest, beneﬁts, claims & demand whatsoever of the Company as amended, varied or supplemented from 
me to me
d) all the tle, interest, beneﬁts, claims and demands whatsoever of the Company in any leer of credit, guarantee, performance 
bond provided by any party to the Company present or future.
e) intangibles, goodwill, uncalled capital , present and future
iii) The term loans were addionally secured by personal guarantee given by one of the director of the Company Mr. Manish 
Dhanuka and one of the director of the holding company Mr. Mahendra Kumar Dhanuka
3) Long term borrowings - 0% Oponally Converble Debentures
During the year ended March 31, 2020, the Company has issued 14,300 0% Oponally Converble Debentures (OCD) of 
Rs.1,00,000 each. In case, the OCD holders exercise their opon to convert the same, then the said conversion shall happen only 
on the basis of face value of each of the OCD and no interest shall be payable to the OCD holders. However, if the OCD holders 
opt not to exercise their opon for conversion, then the OCL holders shall be entled to redempon premium of atleast 11 % IRR 
on annual basis on the amount of the said OCDs or such higher amount as the Board decides aer considering the market price 
of  shares of the Company; however in any case, redempon premium shall not exceed beyond 18% IRR on an annual basis. The 
said OCD, ll the me it is not converted into equity shares, shall not be listed on any stock exchange in India and are permied 
to be transferred only with the permission of the Board of Directors of the Holding Company.  Further there shall be no 
redempon of OCDs, including payment of interest/ other kind of return of what so ever nature thereon, unl enre 
outstanding of the loan availed from Union Bank of India is paid in full to the lender.

present rate of interest is 8.25%. to 8.91%  per annum.Both the facilies have been availed by the Holding Company

Capital management
The Group manages its capital to ensure that enes in the Group will be able to connue as going concern, while 
maximising the return to stakeholders through the opmisaon of the debt and equity balance.
The Group determines the amount of capital required on the basis of annual operang plans and long-term product and 
other strategic investment plans. The funding requirements are met through equity, long-term borrowings and other 
short-term borrowings.
For the purposes of the Group's capital management, capital includes issued capital, share premium and all other equity 
reserves aributable to the equity holders.

Financial risk management objecves
The treasury funcon provides services to the business, co-ordinates access to domesc and internaonal ﬁnancial markets, 
monitors and manages the ﬁnancial risks relang to the operaons through internal risk reports which analyse exposures by 
degree and magnitude of risks. These risks include market risk (including currency risk, interest rate risk and other price risk), 
credit risk and liquidity risk.
The Group seeks to minimise the eﬀects of these risks by using natural hedging ﬁnancial instruments and forward contracts 
to hedge risk exposures. The use of ﬁnancial derivaves is governed by the Group's policies approved by the board of 
directors, which provide wrien principles on foreign exchange risk, the use of ﬁnancial derivaves, and the investment of 
excess liquidity. The Group does not enter into or trade ﬁnancial instruments, including derivave ﬁnancial instruments, for 
speculave purposes.
Market risk
Market risk is the risk of any loss in future earnings, in realizable fair values or in future cash ﬂows that may result from a 
change in the price of a ﬁnancial instrument. The Group's acvies expose it primarily to the ﬁnancial risks of changes in 
foreign currency exchange rates and interest rates. The Group acvely manages its currency and interest rate exposure 
through its ﬁnance division, wherever required, to migate the risks from such exposures.
Foreign currency risk management
The Group undertakes transacons denominated in foreign currencies; consequently, exposures to exchange rate 
ﬂuctuaons arise. The Group acvely manages its currency rate exposures through a centralised treasury division and uses 
natural hedging principles to migate the risks from such exposures.
Disclosure of hedged and unhedged foreign currency exposure
The carrying amounts of the Group's foreign currency denominated monetary assets and monetary liabilies at the end of 
the reporng period are as follows:

Foreign currency sensivity analysis
Movement in the funconal currencies of the various operaons of the Group against major foreign currencies may impact the Group's 
revenues from its operaons. Any weakening of the funconal currency may impact the Group's cost of imports and cost of borrowings 
and consequently may increase the cost of ﬁnancing the Group's capital expenditures. The foreign exchange rate sensivity is calculated 
for each currency by aggregaon of the net foreign exchange rate exposure of a currency and a simultaneous parallel foreign exchange 
rates shi in the foreign exchange rates of each currency by 2%, which represents management's assessment of the reasonably possible 
change in foreign exchange rates. The sensivity analysis includes only outstanding foreign currency denominated monetary items and 
adjusts their translaon at the period end for a 2% change in foreign currency rates.
In management's opinion, the sensivity analysis is unrepresentave of the inherent foreign exchange risk because of the exisng 
exchange earning capacity of the Group on account of its EOU status (Export oriented undertaking) and higher proporon of earnings in 
foreign exchange through exports.
Interest rate risk management
The Group is exposed to interest rate risk because it borrows funds at both ﬁxed and ﬂoang interest rates. The risk is managed by the 
Group by maintaining an appropriate mix between ﬁxed and ﬂoang rate borrowings. Hedging acvies are evaluated regularly to align 
with interest rate views and deﬁned risk appete, ensuring the most cost-eﬀecve hedging strategies are applied. Further, in 
appropriate cases, the Group also eﬀects changes in the borrowing arrangements to convert ﬂoang interest rates to ﬁxed interest rates.
Interest rate sensivity analysis
The sensivity analysis below have been determined based on the exposure to interest rates for non-derivave instruments at the end of 
the reporng period. For ﬂoang rate liabilies, the analysis is prepared assuming the amount of the liability outstanding at the end of 
the reporng period was outstanding for the whole year. A 25 basis point increase or decrease is used when reporng interest rate risk 
internally to key management personnel and represents management's assessment of the reasonably possible change in interest rates.
If interest rates had been 25 basis points higher/lower and all other variables were held constant, the Group's proﬁt for the year ended 
March 31, 2024 would decrease/ increase by Rs. 3.57 lakhs (March 31, 2023 : Rs.55.09 lakhs). This is mainly aributable to the Group's 
exposure to interest rates on its variable rate borrowings.
Credit risk management
Credit risk arises when a customer or counterparty does not meet its obligaons under a customer contract or ﬁnancial instrument, 
leading to a ﬁnancial loss. The Group is exposed to credit risk from its operang acvies primarily trade receivables  and from its
2742023-24
Annual Report
[[PAGE 278]]
₹ in lakhs
Notes to the ﬁnancial statements as at and for the period ended March 31, 2024
(All amounts are INR lakhs, except share data and unless otherwise stated)
ﬁnancing/ invesng acvies, including deposits with banks and foreign exchange transacons. The Group has no signiﬁcant 
concentraon of credit risk with any counterparty.
Exposure to credit risk
The carrying amount of ﬁnancial assets represents the maximum credit exposure. The maximum exposure is the total of the carrying 
amount of balances with banks, short term deposits with banks, trade receivables, margin money and other ﬁnancial assets excluding 
equity investments.
(a) Trade Receivables
The Group has credit evaluaon policy for each customer and based on the evaluaon, credit limit of each customer is deﬁned. 
Wherever the Group assesses the credit risk as high, the exposure is backed by either bank guarantee/leer of credit or security 
deposits.
The Group does not have higher concentraon of credit risks to a single customer. As per simpliﬁed approach, the Group makes 
provision of expected credit losses on trade receivables using a provision matrix to migate the risk of default in payments and 
makes appropriate provision at each reporng date wherever outstanding is for longer period and involves higher risk.
(b) Investments, Cash and Cash Equivalents and Bank Deposits
Credit Risk on cash and cash equivalents, deposits with the banks/ﬁnancial instuons is generally low as the said deposits have 
been made with the banks/ﬁnancial instuons, who have been assigned high credit rang by internaonal and domesc rang 
agencies.
Investments of surplus funds are made only with approved banks/ ﬁnancial instuons/ counterparty. Investments primarily 
include bank deposits, etc. These bank deposits and counterpares have low credit risk. The Group has standard operang 
procedures and investment policy for deployment of surplus liquidity, which allows investment in bank deposit and restricts the 
exposure in equity markets.
Oﬀseng related disclosures
Oﬀseng of cash and cash equivalents to borrowings as per the bank agreement is available only to the bank in the event of a 
default. Group does not have the right to oﬀset in case of the counter party's bankruptcy, therefore, these disclosures are not 
required.
Liquidity risk management
Liquidity risk refers to the risk that the Group cannot meet its ﬁnancial obligaons. The objecve of liquidity risk management is 
to maintain suﬃcient liquidity and ensure that funds are available for use as per requirements. The Group invests its surplus 
funds in bank ﬁxed deposits, which carry minimal mark to market risks. The Group also constantly monitors funding opons 
available in the debt and capital markets with a view to maintaining ﬁnancial ﬂexibility.

repayment periods. The tables have been drawn up based on the undiscounted cash ﬂows of ﬁnancial liabilies based on the earliest 
date on which the Group can be required to pay.

48 Rerement beneﬁt plans
Deﬁned contribuon plans 
Deﬁned beneﬁt plans 
(a) Gratuity
In accordance with Indian law, eligible employees of the Group are entled to receive beneﬁts in respect of Gratuity fund, a
deﬁned contribuon plan, in which both employees and the Group make monthly contribuons at a speciﬁed percentage of the
covered employees’ salary. The contribuons, as speciﬁed under the law, are made to the Provident fund, Gratuity fund,
Superannuaon fund as well as Employee State Insurance Fund. 
The total expense recognised in proﬁt or loss of Rs.393.63 Lakhs (for the year ended March 31, 2023: Rs. 424.91  Lakhs) represents 
contribuon paid to these plans by the Company at rates speciﬁed in the rules of the plan.
Gratuity is payable as per Payment of Gratuity Act, 1972. In terms of the same, gratuity is computed by mulplying last drawn
salary (basic salary including dearness Allowance if any) by completed years of connuous service with part thereof in excess of
six months and again by 15/26. The Act provides for a vesng period of 5 years for withdrawal and rerement and a monetary
ceiling on gratuity payable to an employee on separaon, as may be prescribed under the Payment of Gratuity Act, 1972, from
me to me.  However, in cases where an enterprise has more favourable terms in this regard the same has been adopted.
These plans typically expose the Group to actuarial risks such as: investment risk, interest rate risk and salary risk.
Investment risk
Interest risk
Longevity risk
Salary risk
The principal assumpons used for the purposes of the actuarial valuaons were as follows:

level

Mortality Indian Assured Lives Mortality (2012-14)
(Ulmate)
The present value of the deﬁned beneﬁt plan liability is calculated by reference to the best
esmate of the mortality of plan parcipants both during and aer their employment. An
increase in the life expectancy of the plan parcipants will increase the plan's liability. 
The present value of the deﬁned beneﬁt plan liability is calculated by reference to the
future salaries of plan parcipants. As such, an increase in the salary of the plan
parcipants will increase the plan’s liability.

Indian Assured Lives Mortality (2012-14)
(Ulmate)
A decrease in the bond interest rate will increase the plan liability. However, this will be
parally oﬀset by an increase in the return on the plan’s debt investments.
The present value of the deﬁned beneﬁt plan liability is calculated using a discount rate
determined by reference to the market yields on government bonds denominated in Indian
Rupees. If the actual return on plan asset is below this rate, it will create a plan deﬁcit.
The esmates of future salary increases, considered in actuarial valuaon, take account of inﬂaon, seniority, promoon and 
other relevant factors, such as supply and demand in the employment market.

 sheet

count at the me of separaon and paid as lumpsum.

Higher than expected increases in salary will increase the deﬁned beneﬁt  obligaon

mortality arion, disability and rerement. The eﬀects of these decrement on the DBO 
depends upon the combinaon salary increase, discount rate, and vesng criteria and 
therefore not very straight forward. It is important not to overstate withdrawal rate because 
the cost of rerement beneﬁt of a short caring employees will be less compared to long service 
employees.

53 Disconnuing operaons
 During the previous year, the Company has completed the sale of Orchid Towers which is previously classiﬁed as disposal group  as 
 per Ind AS 105.  Accordingly the gain  of Rs. 3921.04 lacs has been disclosed in exceponal items in  proﬁt and loss account.

per Ind AS 105 "Non Current Assets held for sale and Disconnued operaons". Further, during the previous year the related 
working capital adjustment as per the Sale Agreement was ﬁnailised and the resultant net oulow amounng to Rs.105.81 lakhs  
has been disclosed under disconnuing operaons .

all operaons that have been disconnued by the end of the reporng period for the latest period presented.

number SEBI/HO/CFD/CMD1/ CIR/P/2020/79 dated May 12,

SEBI/HO/CFD/CMD2/CIR/P/2021/11 dated January 15,

“the Circulars”), companies are permied to conduct the 
Annual General Meeng through Video Conferencing / 
Other Audio Visual Means (“VC” / “OAVM”) without the 
physical presence of members at a common venue. Hence, in 
accordance with the Circulars, provisions of the Companies 
Act, 2013 (“the Act”), and SEBI (Lisng Obligaons and 
Disclosure Requirements) Regulaons, 2015 (“Lisng 
Regulaons”), the Annual General Meeng (“AGM”) of the 
members of the Company, for the ﬁnancial year ended on 
March 31, 2024, is being held through VC /OAVM facility on 
Wednesday, August 21, 2024 at 11:30 A.M. (IST). Hence, the 
Members can aend and parcipate in the AGM through VC 
/ OAVM only. In accordance with the Secretarial Standard-2 
on General Meeng issued by the Instute of Company 
Secretaries of India (ICSI) read with guidance/ clariﬁcaon 
dated April, 15, 2020 issued by ICSI, the proceedings of the 
AGM shall be deemed to be conducted at the Registered 
Oﬃce of the Company which shall be the deemed venue of 
the AGM. The detailed procedure for parcipang in the 
meeng through VC / OAVM is appended herewith and also 
available at the Company's website
        hp://www.orchidpharma.com/

aendance of Members has been dispensed with. 
Accordingly, the facility for appointment of proxies by the 
Members will not be available for the AGM and hence the 
Proxy Form and Aendance Slip are not annexed to this 
Noce.

Item No. 8 – Approval for Material Related Party Transacons 
with M/s Otsuka Chemicals (India) Private Limited
Pursuant to the Provisions of Secon 188 of the Companies Act, 
2013 (“the Act”), read with the Companies (Meengs of Board 
and its Powers) Rules, 2014 ('Rules'), the Company is required to 
obtain consent of the Board of Directors and prior approval of the 
members by way of Ordinary resoluon, in case certain 
transacons with related pares exceeds such sum as speciﬁed in 
the said Rules. The aforesaid provisions are not applicable in 
respect of transacons which are in the ordinary course of 
business and on arm's length basis.
However, pursuant to Regulaon 23(4) of the Securies and 
Exchange Board of India (Lisng Obligaons and Disclosure 
Requirements) Regulaons, 2015 (the 'Lisng Regulaons') 
approval of the members through resoluon passed at General 
Meeng is required for all Material related party transacons, 
even if they are entered into in the ordinary course of business 
and on arm's length basis. For this purpose, a transacon is 
considered material, if the transacon/transacons to be 
entered into individually or taken together with previous 
transacons during a Financial Year exceeds rupees one 
thousand crore or 10% of the annual consolidated turnover of the 
Company, as per the last audited Financial statements of the 
Company, whichever is lower.
M/s. Otsuka Chemicals (India) private Limited is a 'Related Party' 
within the meaning of the Companies Act, 2013 and the SEBI 
(Lisng Obligaons and Disclosure Requirements) Regulaons, 
2015. The Company has exisng transacons with M/s. Otsuka 
Chemicals (India) private Limited, which is in the ordinary course 
of business and at arm's length basis. Otsuka Chemical (India) 
Private Limited is the only approved source of the Key Raw 
Material GCLE for Orchid, even before the takeover by Dhanuka 
group. It is only incidental that aer Dhanuka's takeover, this 
transacon has become a related party transacon by virtue of 
Mr. Manish Dhanuka, Managing Director of Orchid Pharma 
Limited being a Director and member in Otsuka Chemical (India) 
Private Limited & Mr. Mridul Dhanuka, Whole Time Director also 
being a Member of Otsuka Chemical (India) Private Limited. 
Moreover, Otsuka also buys the end product produced from GCLE 
for its global requirements from Orchid under a long-term supply 
contract. The proposed RPTs with Otsuka will help the Company 
achieve economies of scale and will be in the best interest of the 
members.
Further, the esmated value of transacons with M/s. Otsuka 
Chemicals (India) private Limited for the ﬁnancial year 2024-25 is 
likely to exceed 10% of the annual consolidated turnover of the 
Company as per the last Audited Consolidated ﬁnancial
